## Analysis Summary

### Comparison Snapshot

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Runs covered | `42` |
| Scored events | `73` |
| Excluded `non-AV` events | `88` |
| Excluded `accidental` events | `141` |
| Overall success rate | `90.4% (66/73)` |
| `unpudo` success rate | `90.9% (60/66)` |
| `unparking` success rate | `85.7% (6/7)` |
| Ownership / handover failures | `14.3% (1/7)` |
| Short AV-attempt failures | `57.1% (4/7)` |
| Successful events with no driver accel help | `100.0% (66/66)` |
| Successful events with route change found | `93.9% (62/66)` |
| Successful gear alignment at anchor | `100.0% (66/66)` |

### Failure Profile

- The scored subset contains `7` failures out of `73` scored events. The dominant effective failure types are `failed_to_unpudo` 28.6% (2/7), `failed_to_slow` 28.6% (2/7), `failed_to_pudo` 14.3% (1/7).
- Ownership loss and handover breakage are the main theme: `1` of `7` failures fall into `not_av_owned`, `completed_outside_av`, `driver_completed_maneuver`, or `interrupted_handover`.
- Route-change evidence inside failed events is `found` in `6`, `not found` in `0`, and `unclear` in `1` cases.
- Source disengagement labels inside failed events are `failed_to_unpudo` 28.6% (2/7), `failed_to_slow` 28.6% (2/7), `failed_to_pudo` 14.3% (1/7); anything else is failing from DBW / ownership / gear evidence rather than an explicit source disengagement label.
- Event-type split: `unpudo` is stronger than `unparking` in this sample: 90.9% (60/66) versus 85.7% (6/7).

### Excluded Events

- `88` events are labelled `non-AV`: the detected maneuver starts outside AV ownership, so they are mentioned for coverage but excluded from model scoring.
- `141` events are labelled `accidental`: AV participates for less than `2s`, so they are treated as accidental contact rather than a meaningful model attempt.

### Success Behavior

- Successful events are distributed as `60` `unpudo` and `6` `unparking`.
- On successful attempts, route change is recovered in `62` of `66` cases and driver accelerator help is absent in `66` of `66` cases.
- Gear state stays aligned at the anchor in `66` of `66` comparable passes. Planned-indicator alignment is `43.9% (29/66)`, and controller-indicator alignment is `45.5% (30/66)`.
- Typical successful timing is median `AV -> event start` `5.70s`, median `AV -> first motion` `5.72s`, and median AV-owned duration `111.06s`.
- Typical successful maneuver shape: common actual gear at the anchor is `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE`, common actual indicator is `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`, and median max AV speed is `5.00 m/s`.

### Written Assessment

This card covers `302` detected events across `42` runs, but only `73` of them are scored AV-owned attempts. The remaining `88` `non-AV` and `141` `accidental` events are explicitly excluded from the success-rate denominator. Within the scored subset, the overall success rate is `90.4% (66/73)`. `unpudo` is stronger than `unparking` in this sample: 90.9% (60/66) versus 85.7% (6/7).

Across the scored failures, route-change evidence is `6` found / `0` not found / `1` unclear, so navigation context is usually present. The failure mix is led by `failed_to_unpudo` 28.6% (2/7), `failed_to_slow` 28.6% (2/7), `failed_to_pudo` 14.3% (1/7), while the excluded portion is mostly `non-AV` starts or sub-`2s` accidental AV contact rather than true scored failures.

When this model succeeds, the behavior is consistent: route change is recovered in `62` of `66` successful events, driver accelerator help is absent in `66` of `66`, and gear alignment holds in `66` of `66` comparable passes. The typical successful event starts moving about `5.72s` after AV engagement, reaches a median max AV speed of `5.00 m/s`, and most often begins in gear `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE` with indicator state `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`.
